# inferred_stub_adjudicated.txt
#
# PRE-SEAT ROM RULINGS. Every line here names a marker-carrying body that was
# disassembled out of the ROM and compared instruction for instruction with its
# src/ body BEFORE it was seated, and records the verdict.
#
# THIS IS NOT AN APPROVAL LIST AND IT IS NOT inferred_stub_debt.txt.
#
#   inferred_stub_baseline.txt  the frozen historical seated set. May only
#                               shrink. Nothing is ever added.
#   inferred_stub_debt.txt      bodies that were ALREADY seated and shipping
#                               when the guard's slice reader was widened and
#                               made them visible. May only shrink, must reach
#                               zero. Nothing is ever added.
#   THIS FILE                   bodies a lane ruled against the ROM and THEN
#                               seated. Entry requires the ruling, and the
#                               guard REFUSES a line that does not carry one.
#
# WHY THIS IS A TIGHTENING AND NOT A LOOPHOLE. The guard's stated fear is that
# "a body that looks right can still be wrong in a way no byte gate on the ROM
# catches, because the ROM never built from this source". The marker is a PROXY
# for that fear -- and a bad one, because it records how the FUNCTION'S NAME
# was recovered, not where the BODY came from. Lane w13 disassembled all 43
# bodies then in the debt queue and ruled 43 REAL DECOMP, 0 guesses. A per-body
# ROM comparison answers the actual question the marker only gestures at, so a
# symbol excused here is held to a STRICTER standard than one on the baseline,
# which was excused by nothing but having already been there.
#
# THE RATCHET IS INTACT. A bare symbol on a line does nothing: the guard parses
# `SYMBOL RULING EVIDENCE` and ignores any line whose ruling is not REAL_DECOMP
# or that carries no evidence reference, so a build cannot be unblocked by
# adding a name. A body that rules GENUINE GUESS or UNVERIFIABLE is not listed
# here at all -- it stays unseated, which is the whole point.
#
# FORMAT:  <symbol>  REAL_DECOMP  <evidence-ref>
#
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# run linkw wave 16 (lane w16-unblock), 2026-08-15.
#
# The four highest-yield targets on the cast ranking (ids 296, 83, 89, 135) were
# refused only because their vtable bodies carry the marker. All 18 were
# disassembled from extracted/overlays/overlay_NNNN.bin at the overlays.yaml
# base -- the shipped image, deliberately not a dsd export, whose relocated
# words would make literals read as live pointers -- and compared instruction
# for instruction against src/. RESULT: 18 REAL DECOMP, 0 genuine guesses, 0
# unverifiable. Register allocation and scheduling differ in places; no
# constant, offset, branch target or instruction count does.
#
# Full per-body evidence: runs/linkw/status/w16.md.
#

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# run link60, lane MG1 (the minigame pathfinder), 2026-08-16.
#
# THE FIRST MINIGAME SCENE. dScMgCurling_c is ov006's curling minigame, actor
# id 0x176, and seating it means seating dScMgBase_c under it -- ov004's
# minigame framework, which every one of the thirty minigame classes derives
# from. Its vtable is THIRTY-SIX slots, not the eighteen the two earlier scene
# seats had, and twenty-five of the thirty-six bodies carried the marker:
# twenty ov004 (the framework's own, slots 1, 2, 5, 7, 10, 12 and 19..32) and
# five ov006 (the class's own overrides, slots 0, 6, 9, 17 and 18).
#
# ALL TWENTY-FIVE WERE DISASSEMBLED AND ALL TWENTY-FIVE RULE REAL DECOMP.
# 0 genuine guesses, 0 unverifiable. Read out of extracted/overlays/
# overlay_0004.bin and overlay_0006.bin at the OVT bases, and for ov006 the
# base was proved rather than assumed: 52 BL sites and 16 pc-relative literal
# sites decoded from the image agree with config/arm9/overlays/ov006/relocs.txt
# with 0 mismatches. (The ov003 and ov007 entries above justify their base with
# "image length equals the OVT ram_size". THAT SENTENCE IS FALSE FOR ov006 --
# the image is 0x80420 and code_size is 0x7eb80, 6304 bytes apart -- so it is
# deliberately not reused here. Every address read is well inside code_size.)
#
# THE TWENTY ov004 RULINGS ARE PAID ONCE FOR ALL THIRTY MINIGAMES. They are
# dScMgBase_c's own slots, and a derived class that does not override a slot
# holds the same word, so the fan-out inherits these rulings rather than
# repeating them. That is the largest single term in port/mg_fanout_costs.txt.
#
# THREE SPELLING DEFECTS were found alongside and are NOT rulings against these
# bodies. They are recorded in hal/scene_mg.cpp and port/mg_fanout_costs.txt
# because each is a live host-ABI or link hazard: src/func_ov006_020e3578.c
# spells ov004's func_ov004_020adc74 as bare func_020adc74, a name in no
# config; include/decl_Scene.h declares Scene::BeforeBehavior with empty parens
# so src/func_ov004_020b0620.cpp's call drops the object pointer; and
# src/func_ov004_020b0840.c calls func_0203cbc0 with no argument while the ROM
# has the pointer being deleted live in r0.
#
# ---- ov004, dScMgBase_c's own twenty ----

# spelling, which is a stronger chain than any earlier lane in this file used:
# relocs.txt from:0x0213e248 kind:load to:0x0213dd40 puts a type_info
# immediately before data_ov006_0213e24c, and relocs.txt from:0x0213dd44
# kind:load to:0x0213dd84 is that type_info's name pointer. 0x0213dd84 reads

#
# All five disassembled with port/tools/w13_dump.py out of
# extracted/overlays/overlay_0006.bin at base 0x020bfec0 -- the shipped image,
# deliberately not a dsd export -- and compared instruction for instruction
# with src/ BEFORE any seating. RESULT: 5 REAL DECOMP, 0 guesses, 0
# unverifiable. No constant, offset, literal or branch target differs on any of
# the five, and the five rulings stand as written.
#
# THREE OF THE INSTRUCTION COUNTS IN THIS BLOCK WERE WRONG WHEN IT WAS FIRST
# WRITTEN, and the correction is left visible rather than quietly applied,
# because this file is read as evidence and a reader is entitled to know which
# numbers were re-derived. The sentence above used to end "or instruction count
# differs on any of the five", which asserted a check that had not been done:
# the counts came from ARITHMETIC ON THE TOTAL DISASSEMBLY LENGTH, which counts
# a function's literal pool words as instructions, and not from splitting the
# body at the first pool address. Found by review, re-derived here:
#
#   symbol      size    words   instructions   pool words   was written
#   ---------   -----   -----   ------------   ----------   -----------
#   021073b0    0x4a8     298            281           17   294  WRONG
#   02107358     0x58      22             21            1    22  WRONG
#   0210730c     0x4c      19             19            0    19  right
#   021042b0     0x38      14             12            2    12  right
#   021071fc    0x110      68             65            3    64  WRONG
#
# instructions + pool words = words = size/4 on every row, which is the check
# the first pass did not have. EVERY POOL-WORD COUNT WAS RIGHT, which is what
# makes the shape of the error legible: the pool was read off the pc-relative
# loads (correctly) and the instruction count was inferred from the total
# (incorrectly), so the two never had to agree. The per-body evidence below --
# the constants, the offsets, the call order, the branch polarity -- was read
# off the disassembly itself and is unaffected.
#
# TWO OF THE FIVE CARRY A WRONG RECOVERED NAME, the same way lane CCN's and
# lane FLW's did: func_ov006_021042b0 is named dScMgPanel_c_OnYoshiTryEat and is
# the deleting destructor, and func_ov006_021071fc is named
# dScMgPanel_c_OnYoshiTryEat_021071fc and is the state reset -- the reset's name

# recoverer telling on itself. Both bodies are exact.

# reproducible:
#
#   arm9 ACTOR_SPAWN_TABLE[381], 0x02090864 + 381*4 = 0x02090e58, holds
#     0x0213d580 in extracted/arm9_dec.bin, and config/arm9/relocs.txt carries
#     the same edge as from:0x02090e58 to:0x0213d580 module:overlays(6,99)
#   SpawnInfo 0x0213d580 = {factory 0x020f8e44, 0x017d017d}, the doubled id
#   typeinfo 0x0213d588 = {0x0209a764, name 0x0213d594, base 0x0213bc64}
#   RTTI string 0x0213d594 reads "13dScMgMCarlo_c"; the base word 0x0213bc64
#     is dScMgSingle3DBase_c's OWN typeinfo, so the ROM states the hierarchy
#   vtable 0x0213d664, whose word at 0x0213d660 is that typeinfo record back
#
# WIDTH 36, FOUR WAYS (section 11's three plus the relocation count):
#   1. SPAN. data_ov006_0213d664 -> next symbol data_ov006_0213d6f4 is 0x90
#      bytes = 36 words exactly, so a 37th slot cannot exist inside the table.
#   2. TERMINAL SLOT. Slot 35 holds 0x020ad660, the family terminal.
#   3. WHAT THE WORD PAST THE END IS. Index 36 reads 0x00000028 -- a small
#      integer with its own config symbol, not a code address.
#   4. RELOCATION COUNT. Exactly 36 load relocations fall inside
#      [0x0213d664, 0x0213d6f4) and none lands on index 36.
#   Section 3's row (36 / 13 / 11 / 0) is confirmed: the 13 overrides against
#   data_ov004_020bc0c0 are slots 0, 2, 5, 6, 7, 9, 10, 16, 17, 18, 19, 26 and
#   33; slots 16 and 33 carry no marker, which is the 11.
#
# EVERY COUNT BELOW CARRIES ITS ARITHMETIC, per the mg6 lane PPP correction:
# instructions + pool words = words = size/4 on every row, computed by
# splitting each body at the LOWEST pc-relative literal address its own
# instructions reference rather than by inferring the instruction count from
# the total.
#
# AND THAT ARITHMETIC CORRECTS TWO EARLIER ROWS IN THIS FILE. The MGA and MGB
# rulings for func_ov006_0210a608 read "0x5c, 17 instructions plus a four-word
# pool" (17+4 = 21, not 23) and for func_ov006_0210a698 "0x4c, 16 instructions
# plus a two-word pool" (16+2 = 18, not 19). Re-derived here the two are 19+4
# and 17+2, which sum to 23 and 19 = 0x5c/4 and 0x4c/4. The per-body EVIDENCE
# in both rows -- the constants, the pool words, the call order, the branch
# polarity -- is unaffected and re-confirms exactly; only the instruction
# counts were low, in the same way and for the same reason lane PPP records.
#
# FIVE OF THE ELEVEN ARE THE 0x0210a6xx FAMILY, ruled before by lanes MGA
# (dScMgCup_c), MGB (dScMgMemory2_c) and FLW (dScMgFlower_c). They are
# re-derived here rather than inherited, because this lane is seating them into
# a FOURTH table, and they are not re-listed as rows: the guard already has
# them and a duplicate line would add nothing. The re-derivation agrees with
# all three earlier ones instruction for instruction (0210a6e4 8+1=9 of 0x24;
# 0210a608 19+4=23 of 0x5c; 0210a698 17+2=19 of 0x4c; 0210a664 13+0=13 of
# 0x34; 0210a600 2+0=2 of 0x8), and the two count corrections above are the
# only difference.
#

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# run mg9 (lane BOO), 2026-08-22. dScMgTeresa_c, actor id 0x183 = scene 387,
# "Hide and Boo Seek".
#
# SIX of this class's eight override bodies carry the marker. All six were
# disassembled out of extracted/overlays/overlay_0006.bin at base 0x020bfec0
# and compared with their src/ bodies BEFORE the vtable fill in
# hal/scene_mg_booseek.cpp was written, and all six rule REAL_DECOMP.
#
# ONLY FOUR GET A LINE HERE, AND THE REASON IS WHAT THIS FILE IS FOR. This file
# excuses bodies that are RULED AND THEN SEATED FROM src. Two of the six are
# not seated from src at all:
#
#   func_ov006_021203fc  slot 0, InitResources. MSVC will not compile the src
#       (C2036, `void *`: unknown size, on the `f2 += 0;` at its line 69), so
#       the body is host-copied in port/unmatched/MgTeresa_InitResources.cpp.
#       RULED REAL_DECOMP anyway, and the ruling is what the host copy was
#       transcribed against: 0x3ac = 235 words = 217 instructions plus an
#       18-word pool, and EVERY pool word is named by the src -- 0x0400000c,
#       0x04000018, 0x00001210, 0x0209d45c, 0x0400000e, 0x0400001c, 0x00009310,
#       0x00000101, 0x00000102, 0x04001008, 0x04001010, 0x00001111, 0x0209d454,
#       0x0400100c, 0x04001018, 0x00000408, 0x020bc880, 0x020bc884. The nine
#       LoadFile ids arrive in ROM order (0x9b, 0x9c, 0x9e, 0x9d, 0x101, 0x102,
#       0x9f, 0xa0, 0xa1) and the two DecompressLZ16 destinations 0x6400000 and
#       0x6600000 are ARM immediates. The last two pool words are the ov004
#       DATA symbols data_ov004_020bc880 and _020bc884, which the src misnames
#       func_020bc880 and func_020bc884 -- a section 6 name-spelling defect
#       that no config defines either half of, and the host copy writes the
#       mount's own symbols instead.
#   func_ov006_021203ac  slot 6, the Behavior. It is the pointer-to-member
#       dispatcher and is host-copied in
#       port/unmatched/MgTeresa_StateDispatch.cpp. RULED REAL_DECOMP: 0x50 = 20
#       words = 19 instructions plus a one-word pool holding 0x02142eb0. The
#       state index is a WORD at this+0x4000+0xbe8 (add r0,r4,#0x4000 then ldr
#       r0,[r0,#0xbe8], an ldr and not an ldrb), then add r3,r1,r0,lsl #3 --
#       an EIGHT-byte stride -- then the five-instruction ARM Itanium sequence
#       verbatim, then func_ov006_0211e4e0 off r4, mov r0,#1, return. The
#       +0x4be8 index cross-confirms slot 0, which sets it to 1, and slot 18,
#       which zeroes it.
#
# THE UNMARKED TWO WERE READ WITH THEM. func_ov006_0211cbd0 (slot 16, D2, 0x24
# = 8 instructions plus one pool word) and func_ov006_021200dc (slot 34, 0x15c
# = 85 instructions plus two) carry no marker and need no line; 0211cbd0 is
# named because its store of data_ov006_0213fa0c is half the cross-confirmation
# slot 17's is the other half, and it matches -- str the table into [this],
# func_ov004_020b29c0(this), return this, which is 0211cbf4 without the
# Deallocate.
#
# instructions + pool words = words = size/4 ON ALL EIGHT, split at the first
# pc-relative load target inside the body rather than inferred from the total
# length, which is the check the PPP block above records getting wrong three
# times out of five.

# word. Both are also CALLED DIRECTLY by this class's own slots 24 and 25, which

# THE COMMAND, RUNNABLE AS WRITTEN. An earlier revision of this block recorded
# it with --c, --func, --addr and --size missing, all four of which match.py
# marks required, so the recipe exited on argparse and reproduced nothing. That
# is a worse failure than no recipe: a reader who tried it would conclude the
# evidence was fabricated rather than that the recipe was. match.py takes ONE
# function per invocation, so it is five invocations and not one sweep:
#
#   cd <worktree root>
#   python tools/match.py --c src/func_ov005_<ADDR>.c \
#                         --func func_ov005_<ADDR> \
#                         --addr 0x<ADDR> --size <SIZE> \
#                         --bin extracted/overlays/overlay_0005.bin \
#                         --base 0x020bfec0 --module ov005 --all --brief

# with (ADDR, SIZE) the five pairs named in the rows below. --module ov005 is
# not optional, and the danger is the OPPOSITE of loud: MEASURED (mg15 MENU
# re-ratify, on func_ov005_020c1a20) -- `--module arm9` reports MATCH,
# `--module ov5` reports MATCH, `--module` omitted reports MATCH; only
# `--module ov006` refuses, and via the WRONG-DEST path, not a clean error.
# A mis-spelled module SILENTLY UNDER-VERIFIES: the byte compare passes and
# the reloc-destination check quietly runs against the wrong (or no) reloc
# table. Spell it ov005 exactly, and confirm the output says the reloc
# destinations were checked. Every row below comes back
# MATCHING at 2004/b56 (the canonical compiler) WITH the reloc destinations
# checked, not merely "bytes match". A body the ROM can be rebuilt from is not
# a behavioural guess, which is the fear the marker stands in for; the marker
# here records how the function's NAME was recovered (from its vtable slot),
# exactly the mis-proxy this file's header describes.
#
# THE EVIDENCE REFERENCE IS AN ABSOLUTE PATH TO A FILE THAT EXISTS. An earlier
# revision cited "runs/mg15/status/MENU.md#ov005", which existed nowhere -- not
# in the run dir, not in the repo tree -- and the guard's parser only checks
# that a third field is PRESENT, so the dangling citation passed silently. That
# is the exact property this file's header claims to protect, so the correction
# is recorded here rather than made quietly.

# run rel0215 wave 1 (lane cast-sweep1), 2026-08-29. OV033's SIX.
#
# Seating level 25's TINY_COVER (104) made six marker-carrying bodies visible
# to this guard at once, because dsd left that class unnamed and so EVERY one
# of its lifecycle slots is a func_ov033_* whose name was recovered from the
# slot. Nothing about the BODIES was ever in doubt; the marker records how the
# NAMES were found, which is the confusion this file exists to settle.
#
# All six were disassembled from extracted/overlays/overlay_0033.bin -- the
# shipped image at the overlays.yaml base, deliberately not a dsd export -- and
# compared instruction for instruction with src/. Then all six were put through
# tools/match.py at 2004/b56 with --strict-relocs, which is a stronger test
# than the eyeball comparison: it byte-compares the compiled output AND checks
# every relocation destination.
#
# RESULT: 6 REAL DECOMP, 0 genuine guesses, 0 unverifiable, 6 MATCHING.
#
# Two things the disassembly settled that the source alone could not:
#
#   func_ov033_021111e4 is labelled "daObjTtFuta_c_OnYoshiTryEat" by the RTTI
#   recovery and it is the DELETING DESTRUCTOR (slot 17). Its literal pool is
#   {0x0211237c own table, 0x0210ae38 Platform base, 0x020a0eac game heap} and
#   its last call is Memory::Deallocate. The ROM parks it at vtable
#   0x0211237c+0x44, the D0 slot. Same mislabel class as func_ov005_020bfefc
#   and func_ov022_021123d0 above.
#
#   func_ov033_02111310's collision-block argument is the pool word at
#   0x0211139c, and config/arm9/overlays/ov033/relocs.txt resolves it to
#   data_ov033_02111bfc -- OV033's OWN symbol. The recovered .cpp spells it

# run rel0215 wave 3 (lane w3-a2), 2026-08-30.
#
# The TEN marker-carrying bodies ov065's Tick Tock Clock cluster seats. They
# were invisible to this guard until now for the reason the guard's own
# docstring gives -- a marker file only counts once a port/hal file NAMES its
# symbol -- and hal/actor_classes_ov065.cpp's seven Ttc fills name all ten at
# once. Every one was disassembled from extracted/overlays/overlay_0065.bin at
# base 0x02115ee0 (the SHIPPED image, deliberately not a dsd export, whose
# relocated words would make literals read as live pointers) and compared
# instruction for instruction with its src/ body: constants, structure offsets,
# branch targets, call sequence and order, instruction count, return value.
# Every literal-pool word was resolved by reading it out of the image at the
# address the ldr computes.
#
# RESULT: 10 REAL DECOMP, 0 genuine guesses, 0 unverifiable. Register
# allocation and scheduling differ in places; no constant, offset, branch
# target or instruction count does.
#
# TWO OF THE RECOVERED NAMES ARE WRONG AND THE BODIES ARE NOT.
# func_ov065_02119f88 and func_ov065_0211abac are headed "OnYoshiTryEat" and
# are plainly deleting destructors -- the same mis-naming the ov022 and ov047
# blocks above record. Do not propagate those names into a header.
#
# AND THE FINDING THAT CHANGED THE SEAT: four of the ten reach their vtables
# and file handles through the placeholders VT0/VT1/G0/G1, which are DEFINED in
# this build (hal/actor_vtables.cpp's `int VT0[20]`, and hal/cxx_aliases.cpp's
# alternatename rows for bare _G0/_G1). Without a per-source -D each of those
# four LINKS CLEAN and writes a dummy array as its object's vptr. The six
# bindings are in port/CMakeLists.txt beside the slice reader, every target
# read out of the body's own literal pool.
#
# Full per-body evidence: runs/rel0215/out/w3-a2/adjudications.txt.
#

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# lane EGG-SLOT30, the Yoshi-egg flight softlock. Vtable slot 30
# (Actor::OnAimedAtWithEggReturnVec) was trapped in 85 of 86 fills, and the
# egg's in-flight state dispatches it on its target EVERY FRAME, so throwing an
# egg at any targetable actor froze the egg in mid-air. The seat is the ROM's
# own arm9 base body 0x020100dc. That base body's one job is to add the result
# of slot 29 (Actor::OnAimedAtWithEgg) to y -- so the two classes whose ROM
# slot 29 is their OWN override had to be seated at the same time, or a seated
# slot 30 would have dispatched straight into a trapped slot 29 and only moved
# the freeze one slot along.
#
# Both bodies are two-instruction constant returns. There is no room for a
# guess in either: the body is fully determined by the two ROM words, and
# match.py reproduces those words from this source on 1.2/base, 1.2/sp2 and
# 1.2/sp2p3 with strict relocs. The marker on each records how the NAME was
# recovered (from the vtable slot), not where the body came from.
#
#   func_ov100_021442d4  ROM: 0000a0e3 1eff2fe1
#                        = e3a00000 mov r0, #0        / e12fff1e bx lr
#                        src: `return 0;`             -- 2/2 words
#   func_ov100_02141fa8  ROM: 820aa0e3 1eff2fe1
#                        = e3a00a82 mov r0, #0x82000  / e12fff1e bx lr
#                        src: `return 532480;` (0x82000 == 130.0 in 20.12)
#                                                     -- 2/2 words
#
# Commands (both, all three configs, MATCH on each):
#   python tools/match.py --c src/func_ov100_021442d4.c --func func_ov100_021442d4 \
#     --addr 0x021442d4 --size 0x8 --module ov100 --trio --strict-relocs
#   python tools/match.py --c src/func_ov100_02141fa8.c --func func_ov100_02141fa8 \
#     --addr 0x02141fa8 --size 0x8 --module ov100 --trio --strict-relocs
#
